1. E -> H -> B -> I -> J
2. E -> F -> K -> X -> G -> H -> B -> I -> J
3. E -> M -> L -> F -> K -> X -> G -> H -> B -> I -> J
4. E -> H -> B -> C -> N -> O -> P -> J

7. E -> F -> K -> G -> H -> B -> C -> N -> Y -> O -> P -> J

Now, we need to find the minimum set of nodes whose removal would eliminate all these paths:

- Removing H would break the paths E -> H -> B -> I -> J and E -> H -> B -> C -> N -> O -> P -> J.
- Removing B would break the paths E -> H -> B -> I -> J and E -> H -> B -> C -> N -> O -> P -> J.
- Removing I would break the path E -> H -> B -> I -> J.

However, removing any one of these nodes (H, B, or I) alone is not sufficient to break all paths. We need to remove at least two nodes to ensure complete disconnection.

Let's consider the combinations:
- Removing H and B would break all paths except the ones that go through F -> K -> X -> G -> H -> B -> I -> J and E -> F -> K -> G -> H -> B -> C -> N -> O -> P -> J. Removing F or K in addition would be redundant as it would not break new paths.
- Removing H and I would break all paths except the ones that go through E -> H -> B -> C -> N -> O -> P -> J. Removing B in addition would be redundant as it would not break new paths.
- Removing B and I would break all paths except the ones that go through E -> H -> B -> C -> N -> O -> P -> J. Removing H in addition would be redundant as it would not break new paths.

The optimal solution is to remove nodes H and I, as this breaks all paths between E and J without needing additional nodes.
